EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
ARREST , BOOKING AND CHARGING REPORTS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2008/09 
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2008/ 09 Annual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) 
conducted an Arrest, Booking, and Charging (A BC) Reports Audit (AD No. C08-003) to 
evaluate arrests for legality and conformance w ith Los Angeles Police Department (Department) 
procedures.   
A
UDIT METHODOLOGY  
 
Audit Division randomly selected a statistically  valid sample of 205 arrest reports from 
Deployment Periods 4 and 5, 2008, and reviewed them  for the stated objectives.  Additionally, a 
separate statistically valid random sample  of 65 Consent Decree (CD) Paragraph ( ¶) 70(b) 
incident arrest packages wa s selected from the same population and reviewed for watch 
commander’s evaluation of the incidents.  
S
UMMARY OF FINDINGS  
 
The table on the following page indicates a summary  of findings as compared to the prior year’s 
audit findings.    
C
ONCLUSION  
 
The Department continues to maintain complian ce on completeness, authen ticity, and legality of 
underlying actions.  Of the five non-compliant objectiv es in the prior audit, each demonstrated an 
increase in compliance rates this year.  Increases  in compliance rates for three objectives (review 
of CD ¶ 70[b] incidents, timeliness of  juvenile telephone calls, a nd post incident supervisory 
review) ranged from 14% to 17%; and, increases in complian ce rates for the remaining two 
objectives (documentation of Miranda responses, and issuance of receipts for property) brought 
the Department into compliance this year.   The only decrease in compliance rates (from 100% in the prior audit to 93% this year) was 
attributable to a lack of documented evidence as to whether medical treatment was provided.   
 The Department appears to have sufficient policies and procedures in place to provide for quality arrest report packages that are compliant wi th CD mandates and Department procedures.  
However, continuous supervisory oversight is essential to ensure De partment policies and 
procedures are followed consistently.  
ii

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL WORK PRODUCT ASSESSMENT SUMMARY  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
First Quarter, Fiscal Year 2008/09 
 
PURPOSE  
 
Pursuant to the Consent Decree (CD) and the Los Angeles Police Department’s (Department) 
Annual Audit Plan for Fiscal Year (FY) 2008/09, Audit Division (AD) has prepared a summary 
(Summary) of the audit reports that contained an element or sample of the Gang Enforcement 
Detail (GED) Work Product (WP) (AD No. C08-012) .  This Summary satis fies the requirements 
specified in CD Paragraph (¶) 131 (a), (f) and (g). 
 This Summary is not an audit, as it is limited to an analysis of certain GED-related findings 
contained within specified audit reports. This Summary is restricted in  scope to the review, 
analysis and reporting of findings identified in  the most recent Consent Decree Audits and 
Command Accountability Performance Audits.  
F
INDINGS  
 
There were no patterns of at-risk practices.  For the 919 non-compliant instances , there were five 
recurrences  in the medium-risk/high-risk rati ng categories.  None of the five recurrences  
evidenced a pattern of at-risk practices.    
 As a whole, GEDs generally complied with Depart ment policies and procedures, the law, and the 
Consent Decree.      
C
ONCLUSION  
 
Although officers are responsible for properly co mpleting reports, gene rally, findings were 
attributable to a lack of consistency in supervisory oversight from both GED and non-GED 
supervisors.  Non-GED supervisors contribu ted to non-compliance rates for GEDs, because 
Department policy precludes GED supervisors fro m approving certain GE D-authored reports.  
However, for those areas for which GED superv isors exercise oversight , there is room for 
improvements.  Since all findings have been repo rted previously and forwarded to responsible 
command staff, no recommendations are offered in this Summary.     
 
The Consent Decree mandates stringent controls for GEDs including selection requirements and 
limited tours of duty.  These controls are effective to assign and ensure accountability in GEDs.   
Additionally, the contro l precluding GED supervisors from  approving certain GED-authored 
reports helps to ensure consistency in supervisor y oversight for GED and non-GED personnel.      
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
COMPLAINT , FORM 1.28, I NVESTIGATIONS AUDIT —P HASE I 
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Second Quarter, Fiscal Year 2008/09 
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2008/ 09 Annual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) 
conducted an audit of Complaint, Form 1.28, Inve stigations Phase I (AD No. C08-009), to assess 
the Los Angeles Police Department’s (Departm ent) compliance with the requirements of 
Consent Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶) 47(g) , 74(d), (f) and (g), 76, 79, 83, 87, 93, 94, 95,  
129(a) and (e), and 152.  
S
UMMARY OF FINDINGS  
 
In the prior audit, the Department did not meet the st andards in five of thirteen objectives.  In this 
year’s audit, the Department did not meet the sta ndards in one of fourteen objectives as indicated 
below.  The Department achieved compliance re garding the timeliness of Internal Affairs 
Group’s (IAG) review of the complaint, the timel iness of the completion of investigations, the 
staffing of IAG investigator positions, and the no tification of complaint intake to the Office of 
Inspector General within one week. 
 
Objective No. 2(c) – Continuation of 24- Hour Toll Free Telephone Complaint Hotline  
 During the prior audit, AD determined that the 24-hour toll-free telephone complaint hotline 
malfunctioned and failed to record every call.  Audit Division recommen ded Internal Affairs 
Group (IAG) contact the Voice Pr int vendor and request the vendor  identify the cause and to 
establish a system that auto matically notifies IAG personnel whenever there is a system 
malfunction.    During this audit, AD personnel called the hot line 30 times within a 46-day period.  On  
12 occasions, either no one answered the telephone or the Voice Print system did not pick up the 
call.      Subsequent to auditing the hotline , IAG indicated the hotline was serviced and working properly.  
Internal Affairs Group indicated  the City’s Information Technology Agency (ITA) conducted a 
diagnostic test on the hotline which revealed that a faulty computer card was in place.  Since ITA 
replaced this card the Voice Print system has b een working properly.  As a result, Civil Rights 
Integrity Division (CRID) conducted additional te sting to determine wh ether the Voice Print 
system answered the calls.  Each of the 14 calls made by CRID personnel was answered by the 
Voice Print system.  Pursuant to an agreemen t with the Independent Monitor, CRID will conduct 
additional testing to determine whethe r the hotline is working properly.  
 The table on the following page illustrates Department compliance rates by objective and 
provides a comparison to the compliance rate s of the Fiscal Year 2007/08 audit.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
WARRANT APPLICATIONS AND SUPPORTING AFFIDAVITS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Second Quarter, Fiscal Year 2008/09 
 
PURPOSE  
 
Pursuant to the United States Department of Justice Consent Decree (CD) and the Los Angeles 
Police Department’s (Department) Annual Audit Plan, Fiscal Year (FY) 2008/09, an audit of 
Warrant Applications and Suppor ting Affidavits (Audit Division No. C08-002) was completed.  
Consent Decree Paragraphs (¶) 62 (b), 71(a), (b) and (c), 72, 128, and 131(a), (c) and (e), outline 
the CD mandates as they relate to the prepara tion, service, and oversight of warrants.   
 
SUMMARY OF FINDINGS

to the prior year’s audit.

ii 
 Executive Summary 
Warrant Applications and Supporting Affidavits Audit 
 
  D
ISCUSSION  
 
The Department met the standards on six of the 14 objectives/sub-objectives , an overall increase 
in compliance from the prior year.  Of the ei ght objectives for which the Department did not 
meet the standards, findings on five objectives were  administrative in nature and determined to 
be low risk.    Findings for the remaining three ob jectives were classified into moderate to high risk categories 
because of the involvement of Department supe rvision and requirements for commanding officer 
review.    Objective No. 2(b) – Inconsistent Information
   
 The majority of inconsistencies were regardi ng documentation of evidence sought and seized. 
 Objective No. 5(c) – Post-Incident Review
 
 Search warrants requiring a Warrant Service/Tact ical Plan Report necessitate a supervisor and a 
commanding officer to conduct an appropriate post -incident review.  Findings were generally 
attributable to a lack of deta ils regarding supervisors’ actions  required on debr iefing summaries 
and commanding officers’ analyses.   
 
Objective No. 7 – Commanding Officer’s Analysis
 
 
The commanding officer is required to complete a Comment Sheet documenting the supervisor’s 
performance at the warrant service.   Findings were genera lly attributable to inadequate analyses 
of supervisors’ actions.    
C
ONCLUSION  
 
Findings were related to defici encies in administrative proces ses, supervisory oversight, and 
commanding officer analysis.  Some findings may be  attributed to a lack of clarity with the 
Department’s current Warrant Service/Tactical Plan Report procedures.  In prior audits, AD 
made recommendations to change some of the pro cedures related to the Warrant Service/Tactical 
Plan Report.    Audit Division reviewed a draf t copy of a special order da ted December 23, 2008.  The draft 
special order would revise procedures related to  the Warrant/Service Tactical Plan Report, and 
relates to both pr ior recommendations and curren t findings.  The draft speci al order proposes that  
Areas/specialized divisions responsible for the se rvice of search and arre st warrants designate a 
Warrant Review Officer in order to minimize administrative deficienci es, and also provides 
guidance for supervisors and commanding officers re garding oversight and an alysis.

 Executive Summary 
Hollywood Area Gang Enforcement Detail Command Accountability Performance Audit    A review of Hollywood Area GED Supervisor Daily Reports indicate d the reports were 
completed in their entirety and appropriatel y documented the GED’s activities.  In addition, 
Hollywood Area GED should be commended, as ther e was a noticeable improvement in both the 
Evaluation of Supervisory Roles and Conduct with a 26 percent increase in meeting the 
standards resulting in an overall  compliance rate of 86 percen t, and the Evaluation of Gang 
Intelligence Information with a 20 percent increas e in meeting the standards resulting in an 
overall compliance rate of 98 percent.  Ho wever, the Hollywood Area GED CAPA identified 
findings in the following area:  
• Evaluation of Work Product, identified inconsistencies contained within an Arrest 
Report’s narrative and booking approval a nd documentation regarding telephone call 
requirements for a juvenile arrestee.  The fi ndings resulted in an overall compliance rate 
of 90 percent with a 2 percen t increase in meeting the sta ndards when compared to the 
prior CAPA.  
 
R
ECOMMENDATIONS /ACTION TAKEN  
 
RECOMMENDATIONS  
 
It is recommended the Commandi ng Officer, Hollywood Area, review  the findings in this audit 
and take appropriate action as follows: 
 
1. Discuss the findings of this audit with Hollywood Division GED and Gang Impact Team 
personnel; 
 
2. Ensure the information contained within th e arrest report narrat ive and booking approval 
forms are consistent and complete;      
 3. Ensure VEAS are completed in their enti rety prior to supervisory approval;   
 4. Provide GED supervision with training re garding originality, as well as timely 
completion, of GED personnel and gang dete ctive performance ev aluations; and,   
 
5. Ensure “Search Warrant Info” boxes at th e bottom portion of the Property Report,  
Form 10.01, are completed. 
 
A
CTION TAKEN  
 
On June 24, 2008, AD briefed the Hollywood Area  Commanding Officer on the audit findings 
and provided him with a copy of the audit report.   The Area Commanding Officer prepared an 
interdepartmental corresponden ce dated June 26, 2008 detailing his response to the audit 
findings.  The Hollywood Area Commanding Officer concurred with the findings based on the 
audit objectives, and indicated he implemented, and will continue to implement, actions and procedures to address and rectify deficiencies.

commanding officers.

 6. Provide GED supervisors with training on using original language when writing 
performance evaluations.

Department resources.

 Executive Summary  
Van Nuys Area Gang Enforcement Detail Command Accountability Performance Audit    Evaluation of Case Categorization met the sta ndards.  One finding under Evaluation of Work 
Product (specifically, the Warrant Tracking Log did not have complete information from the one 
search/Ramey warrant reviewed) resulted in a 2 pe rcent decrease when compared to the previous 
CAPA, resulting in a 94 percent overall performance achievement. 
 
 
R
ECOMMENDATIONS /ACTION TAKEN  
 
RECOMMENDATIONS  
 
It is recommended the Commanding Officer, Van Nuys Area, review  the findings in this audit 
and take appropriate action as follows:  1. Discuss the findings of this audit with Van Nuys Division GED and Gang Impact Team 
personnel; 
 2. Ensure that all VEAS are completed in th eir entirety prior to supervisory approval; 
 
3. Provide supervisory personnel with training regarding the originality of documentation 
within performance evaluations;  
4. Ensure that the Approving Supervisor init ials each page of the search warrant and 
affidavit, as outlined in the Search Warrant Tracking Log, per Special Order No. 28, dated July 15, 2008;   
5. Ensure that the Warrant Tracking Log is accurate and complete; and, 
 
6. Ensure that gang arrest and/ or contact information for each arrest is entered into the 
required Department resources.  
A
CTION TAKEN  
 
 On July 10, 2008, AD attended a meeting with the Van Nuys Area Commanding Officer (C/O) 
to discuss the audit findings and provide him w ith a copy of the audit report.  On July 16, 2008, 
the Van Nuys Area C/O responded by interdep artmental correspondence, which stated, “A 
review of both the officers and s upervisors involved with the identifi ed deficiencies reflected that 
none of them had any previous i ssues in the identified areas.  Van Nuys responded by correcting 
the deficiencies and completing Comment Cards on the identifie d personnel.   
 “The Consent Decree Coordinato r will continue to provide Ro ll Call training and audit updates 
to Watch Commanders, Supervisors, Detectives and officers to ensure that Van Nuys Area 
remains above the compliance rate for mandated pr ovisions of the Consent Decree.  This audit 
was also discussed in detail at the Van Nuys Area Supervisors meeting on July 10, 2008.”
